# Topic definitions and source evidence

Generated by `scripts/phase2_report.py` from [normalized evidence](../normalized_topics.yaml). Edit structured evidence, then regenerate.

This is Phase 2 evidence organization. It assigns no redesigned-course role, mastery, delivery or inclusion decision.

Physical PDF page numbers are 1-based. Book references additionally show printed pages. Claims are paraphrased; page spans may contain several distinct concepts. Read the topic definition and any qualification before inferring equivalence.

Per-topic citations link to source artifacts. Hashes, retrieval times and extraction status are in [corpus.json](corpus.json); offering and original provenance remain in the linked manifests. `direct_catalog_listing` supports a pointer or stated task, not full review of the linked paper.

<a id="rl_formulation"></a>

## Reinforcement learning formulation

ID: `rl_formulation`. Sequential interaction with evaluative feedback; distinguish prediction, control, planning and supervised learning.

Aliases: none recorded.

**Book:** [§1.1, pp.1–3](../../sources/RLbook2020.pdf#page=23) (direct); [§1.3, pp.6–7](../../sources/RLbook2020.pdf#page=28) (direct). Book relation only; does not imply this course assigned or taught it.

- Provisional `/weekly_plan/0/topics/0` (provisional): sequential decision making
- Provisional `/weekly_plan/0/topics/1` (provisional): agent-environment interaction
- Provisional `/weekly_plan/2/topics/0` (provisional): RL vs planning

## Markov decision processes

ID: `mdp`. State, action, transition, reward and discount structure, including the Markov property.

Aliases: MDP, Markov decision process.

**Book:** [§3.1, pp.47–53](../../sources/RLbook2020.pdf#page=69) (direct). Book relation only; does not imply this course assigned or taught it.

- Provisional `/weekly_plan/0/topics/4` (provisional): actions
- Provisional `/weekly_plan/0/topics/9` (provisional): Markov property
- Provisional `/weekly_plan/0/topics/10` (provisional): MDP components

<a id="state_representation"></a>

## State and feature representation

ID: `state_representation`. Agent state, observations, abstraction and representational sufficiency; not a claim of full observability.

Aliases: none recorded.

**Book:** [§17.3, pp.464–469](../../sources/RLbook2020.pdf#page=486) (direct). Book relation only; does not imply this course assigned or taught it.

- Provisional `/weekly_plan/0/topics/2` (provisional): observations vs states
- Provisional `/weekly_plan/0/topics/3` (provisional): state abstraction
- Provisional `/weekly_plan/4/topics/1` (provisional): representation and observability
- Provisional `/assessment_hypotheses/assignments/1/text` (non-binding): Linear approximation, representation limitations, divergence/deadly-triad demonstration, DQN with replay/target network (representational sufficiency - linear works; representational insufficiency - linear does not work but a nonlinear approximator does; algorithmic instability - the representation is not the problem, but the learning dynamics diverge.)

ID: `self_play_game_theory`. Multi-agent best responses, game equilibria, self-play and adversarial search; not synonymous with single-agent MDP control.

Aliases: none recorded.

**Book:** [§16.1, pp.421–426](../../sources/RLbook2020.pdf#page=443) (direct); [§16.6, pp.441–450](../../sources/RLbook2020.pdf#page=463) (direct). Case studies establish self-play; they do not substitute for a full game-theory treatment.

Assessment: unknown; project relationship: unknown.
